# Campaign of 2026-10-03, campaign tier, mcp arm, sufficiency, four probes

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:81d5f62acb289022175d192df0a61faf5f71eb4b79e8dd1f4efae4532ebdd916
lock: sha256:b768fe795c8d44a882e8604a5012c2eb5f2b4d584e7445367dfb24bb21c3585d
selection: sha256:e259547ce18ab03325c4ba1efa9d0db2c6dafa32e2c03120bd9572a00c12077f
read_set: sha256:896882707d477bcbe0d695d228f7d2396b93bc66244dcc478733307bb54c24cb
seed: 0
harness: 0.5.0
tier: campaign
arm: mcp
at: 2026-10-03
cost_cents: 6604
```

**This line is one of the paid layer campaign that [#1659](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1659) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it as line 6 of the plan of batch A, a plan that draws its lines from `tools/probe/layer-campaign.spec`. It is the mcp arm of the campaign tier over the sufficiency selection, on the four probes, at 30 repetitions per probe, on `claude-sonnet-5` under Claude Code 2.1.288 with a cap of 80 turns for each session. The two probes whose leak string stays in the present arm run on the other sufficiency line, and are never pooled into this one.

**One tree, with one move of the pin.** Every session ran in a fresh copy of a `git archive` of the pinned tree, and the engine of that tree. The pin was `682c1e2a` at the start of the batch. It moved to `debe0c70` at session 650 of the batch, at 2026-10-04T09:00:26Z, and the sessions after it ran on `debe0c70`. The move changed five files under `tools/probe/` and no document, and the assembly accepted it. The `tree` digest in the identity above is the digest the assembly recorded, and a reader should not take it to state that all 2150 sessions of the batch read one pin.

**What the recorder counted.** The line holds 120 sessions and cost 6604 cents. The intent hook was live in 120 of them. None stopped at the turn cap, and none at the session budget. The recorder reports 24 paths outside the workspace named in a call of this line, 0 sessions uncounted, and 0 web-tool calls.

**The contract of this batch.** The sessions ran under the recorder contract of spec 15: a `bwrap` sandbox with no network of its own, an empty configuration directory, `--setting-sources project,local`, the permission mode `dontAsk`, and `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` denied. The batches of 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-30 ran under `bypassPermissions` and the configuration of the host, so a rate from this batch does not compare with a rate from those batches.

**Where the figures are.** The rates, the intervals and the comparisons of this line are in the generated result that reads this transcript, and this document states none of them.

      argument: "{\"command\":\"ls /var/tmp/hw-1659/a/ws/L6-campaign-mcp-p3-r1/docs/obligations | tail -20\",\"description\":\"List highest-numbered obligation docs\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Read"
      argument: "/var/tmp/hw-1659/a/ws/L6-campaign-mcp-p3-r1/docs/obligations/0128-nothing-holds-a-crate-to-having-a-contract-under-a-root-that-excludes-it.md"
      result: "sha256:cb7212b334dfe33ec2b53752179604aa75061adcf2983556034165a43daa0c9a"
    - tool: "mcp__headwater__kinds"
      argument: "{\"format\":\"text\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Skill"
      argument: "{\"skill\":\"headwater-authoring\",\"args\":\"Draft a new obligation_record under docs/obligations/ for this gap: nothing in this repository's corpus states how much of a session's token/cost budget the standing instructions of CLAUDE.md (the worktree requirement, the rule table, the skill-load table, etc.) consume before any work starts.     - tool: "Skill"
      argument: "{\"skill\":\"headwater-authoring\",\"args\":\"Write a new process_obligation document under docs/process/obligations/. Context: CLAUDE.md's standing instructions for this repository (worktree entry, skill-load table, the authoring/merge-driver setup, etc.) are read into every agent session before any work starts, consuming part of that session's token/context budget, but nothing in the corpus states how much. The obligation to record: nothing measures or states the session-budget cost that this repository's standing instructions (CLAUDE.md) impose before any work starts, so a later reader/session has no figure to weigh against the value rule or against context limits. This is analogous in spirit to existing entries like docs/process/obligations/0211 (hand remedy lines in hw-run-policy may no longer earn their cost on every dispatch) and 0206 (a named workaround with a per-dispatch cost) — a per-dispatch/per-session overhead that is unmeasured. Use `headwater new process_obligation --title \\\"<title>\\\"` (or the equivalent flow this skill uses) to mint the identifier and scaffold the file, fill in the required facets (status, status_since, summary, last_verified, title, waiting_on) and the required sections (Context, Obligation, Discharge), following the title and prose style of existing docs/process/obligations entries (terse, concrete, no spec numbers in the title, states the gap as a factual claim). Run `headwater check` afterward and report the result.\"}"
      result: ""

    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find discharge relation vocabulary for obligations\",\"prompt\":\"I'm working in the Headwater repository at /var/tmp/hw-1659/a/ws/L6-campaign-mcp-p4-r1 (read-only research, do not edit anything).\\n\\nI need to find what relation this repository's taxonomy declares for \\\"a document discharges an obligation\\\" — i.e., when some document (evaluation, decision, etc.) is the thing that satisfies/closes an obligation (HW-OBL-xxxx), what is the exact relation name used in front matter `relations:` blocks to record that link? Is it called `discharges`, `resolves`, `closes`, or something else? Is it a relation on the obligation side (obligation declares it was discharged_by X) or on the discharging document's side (document declares discharges: HW-OBL-xxxx)?\\n\\nPlease investigate:\\n1. Look at docs/spec/02-taxonomy-model.md for \\\"the decision relation vocabulary\\\" and the full list of relation names the taxonomy declares (governs, traces_to, discharges, supersedes, etc.)\\n2. Search docs/obligations/*.md front matter for examples of relations involving discharge — e.g. grep for \\\"discharge\\\" across docs/, and also grep for \\\"discharged\\\" in obligation files' relations blocks.\\n3. Look specifically at docs/obligations/0104-a-governs-edge-reaches-the-path-it-names-and-nothing.md and docs/obligations/0105-*.md (whichever file number covers HW-OBL-0105 mentioned as discharged in the evaluation file) to see their front matter and relations.\\n4. Check .headwater/taxonomy.yml or taxonomy-source/ for the formal declaration of a \\\"discharges\\\" (or similarly named) relation, including its direction (which kind declares it, which kind it targets) and any constraints (e.g., is it symmetric/inverse of another relation like \\\"discharged_by\\\").\\n5. Look at any other evaluation or decision document that already discharges an obligation, to see the exact front-matter pattern used (key name, list format, which side of the edge it's declared on).\\n\\nReport back: the exact relation key name, which document type declares it (the obligation or the discharging document), the direction/semantics, and a concrete example front-matter snippet from an existing document that uses it. Keep the report under 400 words, with file paths and line numbers as evidence.\"}"
